                      “उǄम खेती मध्यम Ëयापार, नीच चाकरी क ुक्कर िनदान” 
That is, farming is considered the best profession in our society, business is

elders have been comparing it with wagging the tail like a dog. 
Agriculture is considered as the best profession in the Indian society. However,

Government offers the Minimum Support Pr ice for 23 crops. But the purchase of

announced Rs. 1962 as the MSP for maize. However, the farmers are not getting this

Demand to change the logo of the National Medical Commission 
 
DR. SANTANU SEN (West Bengal) : Mr. Deputy Chairman, Sir, the country as a 
whole and the entire medical fraternity de mand the restoration of previous logo of 
National Medical Commission.  Sir, earlier it was Indian Medical Degree Act, 1916, 
which became Indian Medical Council Act, 1933 and finally it became the Indian 
Medical Council Act, 1956. Thereafter, de spite having several objections from 
different corners of the society and the fraternity, it was repelled and the National 
Medical Commission came into force from 25 th September, 2020 repealing the 64 
years old Indian Medical Council Act, 1956.  Sir, earlier it was termed as Western 
Medicine, then it became Medicine and fina lly, it was termed as Modern Medicine.  
The logo of Modern was caduceus.  There was no need and there was no permissible 
condition so far as the National Medical Commission is concerned that this logo can 
be changed, but, unfortunately, we have  seen in the recent past, I don’t know 
whether it is because of the Government's  instruction or by the National Medical 
Commission itself, they have changed the logo and they have brought a photograph 
of Dhanvantari in the logo.  Sir, the chan ge of logo was absolute ly not needed.  It 
symbolizes one particular religion.  What is the function of the National Medical 
Commission? It regulates the medical curriculum; it gives sanction to the new medical 
colleges. Its function is not to promote any particular religion.  Even the AYUSH 
Department did not change its logo. But, the National Medical Commission has 
changed its logo. It is against the basic oath which we, the doctors, take after 
passing MBBS, be it the Charak oath or be it the Hippocratic Oath.  We take oath that 
we will treat each and every patient irrespective of their caste, creed and religion.  We 
are not bound to treat one pa rticular religion. Sir, again, it is against the basic 
essence of the Indian Constitution which, through its Articles 25 and 26, after the 42
nd 
Amendment in 1976, speaks that India is a secular country and we should promote 
dovetailing of religion.  There are eight majo r religions of the world that very much 
exist in our country.  We know, by now,  we worship Lord Ram and some use the 
religion for their political benefit. It is know n to all that in Uttar Pradesh, one Dalit is 
forced to lick his spit from ground b ecause his son gets married to a woman who 
belongs to another community.  And, it is kn own to us by now that in the Lok Sabha, 
their MP openly abuses one Muslim MP

Rising cases of crimes against women 
 
SHRI ABIR RANJAN BISWAS (West Bengal) : Mr. Deputy Chairman, Sir, I thank you

[ 11 December, 2023 ]
25

 S i r ,  I  w i s h  t o  b r i n g  t o  y o u r  n o t i c e  that recently the National Crime record 
Bureau (NCRB) has reported revealing a staggering figure of 4,45,256 cases of 
crimes committed against women in 2022. This  translates into about 51 FIRs filed 
every hour! The issue of the safety of women has become very paramount. It is 
imperative for authorities to address this  concerning trend and implement robust 
measures for ensuring safety and security of women all across the nation. 
 While the data underscores the urgent need to have comprehensive strategies 
to combat gender-based crimes, it is noteworthy that Kolkata stands out as the 
safest city in the country.  This distin ction highlights the effectiveness of local 
initiatives and law enforcement efforts in creating a secure environment for women. 
 Sir, to further enhance women safety na tionwide, it is crucial for policy-makers 
to analyze successful models like that of Kolkata and replicate best practices available 
all over the nation.  This may includ e increased police presence, community 
engagement programmes and leveraging tec hnology for a rapid response to such 
incidents. 
 This alarming statistics underscores th e responsibility of both, law enforcement 
and roping in and engaging the community to work collaboratively in tandem in 
creating a society where women can live without fear.   
 Sir, it is essential to prioritize and invest in women’s safety, ensuring that every 
city and region becomes a safe haven for women to thrive and contribute to the 
nation’s progress. Thank you.

Demand to include '
Ahimsa' in Preamble of the Indian Constitution 
 
DR. SASMIT PATRA (Odisha): Hon. Deputy Ch airman, Sir, thank you for giving me 
this opportunity on this very important topic. The topic is, ‘Demand to include Ahimsa 
in the Preamble of the Indian Constitution’ as proposed by hon. Chief Minister, Shri 
Naveen Patnaikji. 
 Sir, in May, 2018, Odisha’s hon. Chief Minister, Shri Naveen Patnaikji, during 
the first meeting of the Committee for 150
th Birth Anniversary Celebrations of Mahatma 
Gandhi had proposed that the idea of ahimsa  or non-violence should be included in 
the Preamble of the Indian Constitution. Hon. Deputy Chairman, Sir, he had said, 
‘this would inspire future generations not on ly of India but also people around the 
world who would be reminded of  the profound truth of this principle.’ He further said 
that ‘this is the most important aspect of Mahatma Gandhi, the doctrine of non-
violence, because it talks about poverty and social injustice as the greatest violence 
against humanity.’  Again in December, 2019, hon. Deputy Chairman, Sir, hon. Chief 
[ 11 December, 2023 ]
31

Minister of Odisha, Shri Naveen Patnaikiji , reiterated his demand for inclusion of 
ahimsa, non-violence, in the Preamble of the Indian Constitution while addressing the 
second meeting of the National Committee for Celebration of 150th Birth Anniversary of 
Mahatma Gandhi at the Rashtrapati Bhavan in  New Delhi, and he said that ‘Ahimsa is 
the most powerful message given by India to the world and that Ahimsa has made our 
freedom struggle a unique freedom struggle in  the entire world while Ahimsa is the 
only path of peace, development and equality.’   
You would be happy to know hon. Deputy  Chairman, Sir, that in March, 2021, 
the Odisha Vidhan Sabha, the Odisha Assembly, unanimously passed a Resolution to 
include Ahimsa, non-violence,  in the Preamble of the Indian Constitution. The 
Resolution was passed to commemorate the 100 th Anniversary of Mahatma Gandhi’s 
visit to the State of Odisha.  He had first visited on March 23 rd, 1921, and, in this way, 
hon. Deputy Chairman, Sir, hon. Chief Mi nister, Shri Naveen Patnaikji, has been 
repeatedly requesting for ‘Ahimsa’ to be  added in the Preamble of the Indian 
Constitution.  The Odisha Vidhan Sabh a, unamimously, all MLAs, both BJP, 
Congress, BJD, Independents, eve ryone came together and said, " अिंहसा को भारत 
क े संिवधान क े ĢीएÇबल मȂ शािमल किरए।" This is beyond party;  this is about the very 
philosophy of India; this is about the very identity of India, which is Ahimsa and non-
violence.   
 In this regard, I reiterate that let the Government bring about a proposal and an 
amendment to include Ahimsa in the Preamble of the Indian Constitution. Thank you.

The issue of Laser Interferometer Gravitational Wave Observatory (LIGO) in Hingoli, 
Maharashtra 
 
DR. FAUZIA KHAN (Maharashtra ):  Sir, as India takes off in to the realms of scientific 
advancement, as a proud Indian, I would like to say: 
 
"न तू ज़मीं क े िलए है, न आसमाँ क े िलए, 
32
[RAJYA SABHA]

जहाँ है तेरे िलए, तू नहीं जहाँ क े िलए।" 
ﻧہ ﺗﻮ ﺯﻣﻴﮟ ﮐﮯ ﻟﻴﮯ ﮨﮯ، ﻧہ ﺍٓﺳﻤﺎں ﮐﮯ ﻟﻴﮯ،† 
ﺟﮩﺎں ﮨﮯ ﺗﻴﺮے ﻟﻴﮯ، ﺗﻮ ﻧﮩﻴﮟ ﺟﮩﺎں ﮐﮯ ﻟﻴﮯ 
 
Sir, India has embarked upon a mome ntous journey, marking its celestial 
prowess by touching the Moon and nearing the Sun.  Yet, our quest to unravel 
cosmic mysteries has only just commenced.  In the heart of Maharashtra's Hingoli, a 
barren expanse of land is poised to become  the ground for one of the world's most 
ambitious scientific initiatives, the LI GO Observatory, dedicated to unraveling 
gravitational waves.  I extend my heartfelt gratitude to the hon. Prime Minister of India 
for swiftly considering the plea that I pe rsonally made to him and granting the 
Government's wholehearted approval and a sanctioned fund of Rs. 2,600 crore for 
LIGO India. 
 Sir, this project promises an impact ful change for the underprivileged in the 
Marathwada region of Maharashtra, making it a big hub for science.  It pledges not 
only extensive scientific research, but al so substantial investments in education, 
healthcare and infrastructure, revitalizing  the entire area. Sir, I implore the 
Government to seize this opportunity by establishing a science city, fostering a world-
class research university and constructing state-of-art infrastructure with robust 
connectivity all around the LIGO Project.  Moreover, I propose the creation of a 
dedicated committee tasked with overseeing  and guiding this monumental project.  
This committee would offer recommendatio ns and strategies to harness this 
opportunity for the comprehensive developm ent of the Marathwada region.  This 
endeavour is not merely about a scientific ex ploration.  It is about fostering a future 
where knowledge, innovation and progress th rive.  Let us unite in our commitment to 
leverage this endeavour for the holistic advancement of our nation and its people.  Sir, 
this is only the third location in the worl d that NASA has identified to set up this 
Laboratory.  Thank you, Sir.

ORAL ANSWERS TO QUESTIONS 
 
Consumption status under PMUY 
 
*76. SHRI SUSHIL KUMAR MODI: Will the Minister of  PETROLEUM AND NATURAL 
GAS be pleased to state: 
 
(a) the number of Ujjwala beneficiaries and to tal refills consumed by them in last 3 
years; 
(b) the per capita consumption of refillin g under Pradhan Mantri Ujjwala Yojana 
(PMUY) in Financial Year 2020 to 2023; 
(c) the number of Ujjawala beneficiaries who took only 1 refill, 2 refills, 3 refills and 
4 refills in last two financial years; 
(d) the steps taken to encourage consumption of LPG by PMUY beneficiaries; and 
(e) the number of LPG bottling plants and distributors increased since 2021?  
 
[ 11 December, 2023 ]
37

THE MINISTER OF PETROLEUM AND NATURAL GAS (SHRI HARDEEP SINGH 
PURI): (a) to (e) A statement is laid on the Table of the House.  
 
Statement 
 
(a) Pradhan Mantri Ujjwala Yojana (PMU Y) was started in May, 2016 with the 
objective of providing access to clean cook ing fuel to poor households across the 
country according to a defined criteria.  Under PMUY, deposit free LPG connections 
are provided to adult women from poor households. As of 30.09.2023, 9.59 Crore 
PMUY connections have been released in th e country. A total of 100.85 crore refills 
were taken by PMUY consumers in last 3 years i.e. FY 2020-21, 2021-22 & 2022-23. 
 
(b) Per capita consumption of PMUY Be neficiaries has increased from 3.01 
Refills/annum (in terms of 14.2 Kg refill) during FY 2019-20, to 3.71 Refills/annum 
during FY 2022-23 & further to 3.8 refills in 2023-24 (till October 2023).   
 
(c) The number of PMUY beneficiaries who took  1 refill, 2 refills, 3 refills and 4 refills 
including installation refill in last two financial years is as below: 
(in lakh) 
Financial Year One refill Two refills Three refills Four refills 
Five & more 
refills 
FY 2021-22 161.5 148.74 130.89 103.62 260.48 
FY 2022-23 155.81 149.24 131.19 104.27 300.28 
Source: Indian Oil Corporation Limited on industry basis 
 
(d) Government has taken several steps to  encourage consumption of LPG, which 
include targeted subsidy of Rs.200/- per 14.2  Kg refill upto 12 refills/year for PMUY 
beneficiaries for years 2022-23 and 2023-24, increase in ta rgeted subsidy to Rs. 300 
per 14.2 kg cylinder for up to 12 refills per annum since October 2023, option of 5 Kg 
Double Bottle Connection (DBC), swap option from 14.2 kg to 5 Kg, upto 3 free refills 
to PMUY beneficiaries under Pradhan Mantri Garib Kalyan Package from April 2020 to 
December 2020 etc. For ease of refill booking various methods have been provided 
viz. Interactive Voice Response System(IVRS), Short Message Service(SMS), 
Whatsapp, calling directly on the phone of  distributor, e-commerce platforms, OMC 
mobile applications, OMCs web-portals etc.  The effective price of domestic LPG

Nepal 1198.56 
#Source: Petroleum Planning and Analysis Cell

To improve LPG usage amongst PMUY benefi ciaries, Public Sector Oil Marketing 
Companies (OMCs) have organized LPG panchayats, social media campaigns, 
public events for the distribution of connections and public awareness campaigns etc. 
across the country.   
 
(e) OMCs continuously expand the distri bution network by commissioning new LPG 
distributorships. Between April 2021 and November 2023, while the number of LPG 
distributorships has increased from 250 83 to 25437, the number of LPG bottling 
plants has increased from 200 to 210.

 Regarding what we did during the curren t Prime Minister's period, I would like 
to inform that we took a number of steps to do it very differently.  We diversified the 
sources of supply.  The hon. Prime Minister, who is very sensitive to the requirements 
of the most vulnerable sections of the so ciety, reduced the Central Excise Duty on 
petrol and diesel on two occasions -- in November, 2021, and in May, 2022.  As a 
result of this reduction in this Excise Duty -- and this is very in teresting -- the loss of 
revenue to the Government was Rs. 2.2 lakh corers.  The BJP-ru led States reduced 
the VAT, and I want to thank them for that . As a result of this, what happened was 
that we had two differential levels of petrol  and diesel pricing. You had the BJP-ruled 
States where petrol, on an average, sells for Rs.96 a litre and you have the non-BJP 
ruled States.  I don't want to name any because that is not, I think, the question, but 
the price difference is about Rs.12 per litre . And, a person never goes to a petrol 
pump to just fill one litre of petrol.  An  average car which a ci tizen uses, or a two-
wheeler or three-wheeler, fills many litres.  So, if you have a car  with 30 litre petrol 
tank and the price differential is Rs.12 per li tre, you will realise what the issue involved

SHRI HARDEEP SINGH PURI: Sir, I am very happy that the hon. Member has asked 
that question. I think there is some misunderstanding.  We have what is called 
eligibility criteria.  The eligib ility criteria consist of seve ral markers. Accordingly, a 
beneficiary can receive benefits  under the eligibility criteria. It is a defined criteria for 
an adult woman belonging to any of the following categories: SC households, ST 
households, Pradhan Mantri Awas Yojana (Gramin), most backward classes, 
Antyodaya Anna Yojana, tea and ex-tea ga rden tribes, forest dwellers, people 
residing in islands or river islands, SECC households and poor households as per the 
14-point declaration, but there should no t be any LPG connection in the household 
other than the one you are applying for. We also have negative criteria. We have what 
is called a KYC form, an undertaking.  This in cludes 14 points. If any beneficiary, after 
signing this form, is found to be in violat ion or in contravention of any of these 14 
points, the connection could be withdrawn.  What we had done earlier was this.  The 
direct benefit to which the hon. Member is referring to is a very simple thing.  Earlier, 
what used to happen was that the consum er would go and pay Rs.1,100 upfront. 
When we introduced subsidy for Ujjwala, it is a direct transfer to the beneficiary's 
account and that direct transfer takes place within 48 hours.  I have never heard any 
complaint. So, those who are entitled to receive the benefit continue to receive it.  It is 
entirely possible that the hon. Member is referring to some households which don't 
qualify according to the criteria .  As I said, there is eligibilit y criteria and then there is 
KYC form. This seems to be working well. I should mention that when the decision 
was taken to provide 75 lakh addition al household connections, 15 per cent 
applications out of those 34 lakh applications received, were rejected because by use 
of Aadhaar card, we found that those households actually had another connection 
within the definition of 'household'.

petrol and diesel have come down in the last  2 years. Changes in prices of petrol and

Prices of petrol and diesel are market de termined and Public Sector Oil Marketing 
Companies (OMCs) take appropriate decision  on pricing of petrol and diesel. While 
crude oil prices (Indian basket) increased from $73.30/bbl (December 2021) to 
$112.87/bbl (March 2022) and further to $116.01 (June 2022) and continue to 
remain highly volatile, OMCs have not increased/revised prices since 6
th April 2022. In 
fact, excise duty reductions done in No vember 2021 and May 2022 of Rs. 13/litre and 
[ 11 December, 2023 ]
47

Rs. 16/litre on petrol and diesel respect ively were fully passe d on to consumers. 
Some state governments also reduced state VAT to provide relief to citizens. 
Government of India also took several other steps to insulate common citizens 
from high international prices, which includ ed diversifying the crude import basket, 
windfall taxes on export of petroleum produc ts, invoking the provisions of Universal 
Service Obligation to ensure availability of  petrol & diesel in domestic market, 
increasing the blending of ethanol in petrol, etc. 
India imports more than 60% of its domestic LPG consumption. Price of LPG 
in the country is linked to its price in the international market. Government continues 
to modulate the effective price to consum er for domestic LPG. During the period 
2020-21 to 2022-23, the average Saudi CP (international benchmark for LPG pricing) 
went up from $415 per MT to $712 per MT . However, the increase in the international 
prices was not fully passed on to the customers. The effective price of domestic LPG 
cylinder in neighboring countries as on 01.12.2023 is as below: 
(In Indian Rupees) 
Country Domestic LPG (Rs./14.2 kg. cyl.) # 
India 603.00* 
Pakistan 1059.46 
Sri Lanka 1032.35 
Nepal 1198.56 
#Source: Petroleum Planning and Analysis Cell 
*Effective cost to PMUY beneficiaries in Delhi 
 
Government has reduced the effective price of domestic LPG by Rs. 200 per 
14.2 Kg LPG cylinder with effect from 30 th August, 2023. Unde r PAHAL Scheme, the 
domestic LPG cylinders are sold at non-subsidised price and the applicable subsidy 
to the consumers is transferred directly in to their bank accounts. OMCs credit the 
subsidy immediately to consumers and he nce there is no pendency of subsidy 
payments. Apart from the direct subsidy to  bank accounts to consumers, the OMCs 
have also been compensated Rs 22,000 crore in FY 2022-23 by Government of India 
to cover the under-recoveries suffered by  them in not passing on the high 
international prices to the domestic LPG consumers. 
W.e.f. 21
st May, 2022, Government has been providing a budgetary support for 
targeted subsidy of Rs. 200 per 14.2 Kg LPG cylinder for Pradhan Mantri Ujjwala 
Yojana (PMUY) beneficiaries for upto 12 refills a year for ye ars 2022-23 and 2023-24. 
Moreover, w.e.f.  5
th October, 2023, the targeted su bsidy has been further increased 
to Rs. 300 per 14.2 Kg LPG cylinder for all Pradhan Mantri Ujjwala Yojana (PMUY)

beneficiaries. As on 01.12.2023, the ef fective price of domestic LPG for PMUY 
consumers is Rs. 603 per 14.2 Kg LPG cylinder (in Delhi).

Beneficiaries under Pm-Svanidhi Scheme 
 
* 78.  SHRIMATI PRIYANKA CHATURVED I: Will the Minister of HOUSING AND 
URBAN AFFAIRS be pleased to state: 
 
(a) whether Government has any data re garding the number of beneficiaries 
under PM-SVANidhi Scheme for the last three years, if so, the details thereof, 
State-wise; 
(b) whether Government has any specific mech anism to identify the beneficiaries 
under the PM-SVANidhi Scheme, if so, the details thereof; 
(c) whether Government has any plans to  increase the credit under the PM-
SVANidhi Scheme, if so, the details thereof; and 
(d) the total amount disbursed to the beneficiaries under the PM-SVANidhi 
Scheme by Government in the last three years, year-wise? 
 
THE MINISTER OF HOUSING AND URBAN AFFAIRS (SHRI HARDEEP SINGH PURI): 
(a) to (d) A statement is laid on the Table of the House. 
 
Statement 
 
(a) Yes, Sir. As on 5 December, 2023, 56.58 lakh street vendor beneficiaries have 
been disbursed loans under Prime Minister  Street Vendor’s AtmaNirbhar Nidhi (PM 
SVANidhi) Scheme since its inception. Th e State/UT-wise details of number of 
beneficiaries for last three years under the scheme are at Annexure-I.  
 
(b) Yes, Sir. All Street Vendors engaged in  vending in urban areas are eligible for 
benefit under PM SVANidhi Scheme, irrespective of the date on which they started 
vending.As per Scheme guide lines, eligible vendors are identified based on following 
criteria: 
i.  Street vendors in possession of Cert ificate of Vending / IdentityCard issued 
by Urban Local Bodies (ULBs); 
ii. The vendors, who have been identified  in the survey but havenot been issued 
Certificate of Vending / Identity Card; 
iii. Street Vendors, left out of the ULB led identifi cation survey orwho have 
started vending after completion of the survey, andhave been issued Letter of 
Recommendation (LoR) to thateffect by the ULB / Town Vending Committee 
(TVC); and 
56
[RAJYA SABHA]

iv. The vendors of surrounding development/  peri-urban / ruralareas vending in 
the geographical limits of the UL Bs and havebeen issued Letter of 
Recommendation (LoR) to that effect bythe ULB / TVC.  
 
(c) Under PM SVANidhi Scheme, a working capital loan upto ₹10,000 was introduced 
initially. Considering the requirement for enhanced loan, 2 nd loan amount upto 
₹20,000 w.e.f  April 09, 2021, and 3 rd loan upto ₹50,000 w.e.f. June 01, 2022 were 
introduced. 
 
(d) The year-wise details of total amount disbursed to the beneficiaries under the 
PM- SVANidhi scheme for last three years are at Annexure-II.

the fact that this scheme started at the he ight of the pandemic when the hon. Prime

the adversity they were facing, we rolled out the guidelines in one day.  In one month, 
the scheme started rolling.  Today we ha ve already got something like 56.89 lakh 
beneficiaries.  I would like to tell the hon. Member that, in fact, we have a portal which 
is upgraded daily.  I was responding to a question in the Lok Sabha, I think a few days

preparing for this Session, I knew the hon.  Member had asked th is Starred Question, 
it has gone up to 56.89 lakh.  I am sure by th e time we finish the day, it will be past 57

the first loan is 56.89 lakhs.  The number of people who have taken the second loan is 
15.82 lakhs.  The number of people who have  taken the third loan is 2.05 lakhs.  Two

fundamental flaws each of which possess a sign ificant threat to the future of Kashmir 
and its people. The most important egregious aspect of the Bill is, it's blatant discreet 
for the principle of representation, a co rnerstone of any democratic system. By 
empowering the Lieutenant Governor to  nominate Members to the Legislative 
Assembly, the Bill effectiv ely strips the people of Jammu and Kashmir of their 
fundamental rights to choose their representa tives. In the history of India, no State 
has been downgraded into a Union Territory especially without the consent of the 
people or their elected representatives. The proposed increase in  the Assembly seats 
slowly to accommodate reservation for SCs an d STs appears to be a positive step on 
the surface. However, it fails to address the underlying structural inequalities and 
systematic discrimination faced by the comm unities. Sir, the Bill's selective approach 
in addressing the issue of displacement, by proposing the nomination of Kashmiri 
migrants, raises serious concern about the exclusion of other communities who have 
suffered similar hardships. This singling ou t of one particular group further deepens 
[ 11 December, 2023 ]
81

existing division and creates an enviro nment of resentment and suspicion among 
different communities. Sir, tr ue progress lies in acknowledging the suffering of all 
displaced communities and actively working towards providing them with justice and 
sense of belonging. The great Dravidian stalwart Kalaignar Anna once said, India is a 
vast diverse country. *

Kashmir Reorganisation (Amendment ) Bill, 2023 and the Jammu & Kashmir 
Reservation (Amendment) Bill, 2023.   
 The Bill seeks to reserve seats for Kash miri migrants, people displaced from 
Pakistan-Occupied-Kashmir (POK) and the Scheduled Tribes.  The reservation of 
seats for Kashmiri migrants, who were disp laced from their own homes in 1990s, will 
ensure that the voice of the community is h eard loud and clear in the J&K Assembly.    
The Bill intends to increase the seats of th e Jammu & Kashmir Assembly from 107 to 
114.  This also introduces a new section,  that is, 15A and vests power with Lt. 
Governor to nominate not more than two Members, one of whom will be woman from 
the community of Kashmiri migrants to the J&K Assembly.  
 Sir, as far as the Jammu & Kash mir Reservation (Amendment) Bill is 
concerned, it is a harmless Bill.  The so cially and educationa lly backward classes 
have definitely to be substituted from weaker and under-privileged classes.  And, that 
should be passed.  
 By virtue of Section 15(B), the Lieute nant Governor of th e Union Territory of 
Jammu & Kashmir may nominate one Me mber from displaced persons from 
Pakistan-Occupied-Kashmir to the J&K Assembly.  You have to get read
 y for an 
election process.  The delimitation process has already been completed.  Elections 
will be conducted at the earliest and a po pulist Government would be put in place.  
Major steps have already been taken to bring normalcy to hold the elections.

DR. SIKANDER KUMAR (Himachal Pradesh) : Thank you, Mr. Vice-Chairman, Sir, 
for giving me the opportunity to participat e in the discussion on two important Bills, 
i.e.  The Jammu and Kashmir Reservatio n (Amendment) Bill, 2023 and the Jammu 
and Kashmir Reorganisation (Amendment) Bill, 2023.  I would like to thank the 
hon'ble Prime Minister and the hon'ble Home Minister to bring these important Bills 
which provide rights to the depressed cl asses of Jammu and Kashmir.  I also 
welcome today's verdict of the hon'ble Supreme Court of India which further 
endorses the decision of the Parliament and the Government of India on abrogation of 
Article 370 and Article 35.  The Jammu and Kashmir Rese rvation (Amendment) Bill, 
2023 was introduced in Lok Sabha on Ju ly 26, 2023 and passed on December 6, 
2023.  It amends the Jammu and Kashmir Reservation Act, 2004.  The Act provides 
reservation in jobs and admission in pr ofessional institutions to members of 
Scheduled Castes, Scheduled Tribes and Ot her Socially and Educationally Backward 
Classes.  Under the Act, Socially and Educationally Backward Classes include people 
residing in villages declared as socially  and educationally backward by the Union 
Territory of Jammu and Kashmir, the people residing in areas adjoining the Actual 
Line of Control and international border  and weak and under-privileged classes 
(social castes), as notified. 
 
 
140
[RAJYA SABHA]

(MR. CHAIRMAN in the Chair.) 
 
 The Government may make inclusions or  exclusions from the category of weak 
and underprivileged classes, on the reco mmendations of a Commission.  The Bill 
substitutes weak and underprivileged cla sses with Other Backward Classes, as 
declared by the UT of Jammu and Kash mir.  The definition of weak and 
underprivileged classes is deleted from  the Act.  The Jammu and Kashmir 
Reorganisation (Amendment) Bill, 2023, ai ms to add new Section 15A and Section 
15B to the Jammu and Kashmir Reorgani sation Act, 2019. These Amendments 
empower the Lieutenant Governor of the Union Territory to nominate not more than 
two Members, one of whom shall be a wo man, from the community of Kashmiri 
migrants, and one Member from the displaced persons from Pakistan-Occupied 
Kashmir, to the Jammu and Kashmir Legislative Assembly.   
 The Jammu and Kashmir Reorganisation (Amendment) Bill, 2023, also 
proposes Amendments to sub-sections (3) and (10) of Section 14 of the Jammu and 
Kashmir Reorganisation Act, 2019.   Th ese changes aim at extending reservation 
benefits to the Scheduled Tribe community in the Legislative Assembly of Jammu and 
Kashmir.   
 Additionally, the Bill seeks to expand the total composition of the Assembly 
from 107 to 114 Members.  The inclusio n of representatives from displaced and 
migrated communities underlines the essen ce of the elected Government practice as 
observed in several Union Territories and so me States.   In this context, the BJP 
Government has expanded the total seats from 107 to 114 encompassing 90 seats for 
the General Category, 7 for Scheduled Castes, 9 for Scheduled Tribes and 3 
designated for nominated Members.  This breakdown underscores the commitment 
to fair representation ensuring that no co mmunity faces unjust treatment.  It reflects a 
balanced approach providing adequate representation to all the communities 
including minority groups such as Hindus and Sikhs.   
 With these words, I support these two Bills and also urge all the hon'ble 
Members to support and pass these Bills.  Thank you, Sir.
